Hi, Doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm explaining this right, and I'm sure it's probably just a bad habit I need to break, but... I've been having some trouble at work.

I know there's more to do, but my mind just kind of wanders off to the next thing before I've actually finished the first thing. \n\nLike, I'll be doing the logs, and I know I need to sign the bottom, but then I'll think about how I need to go check the temperature on the cooler, and I just... walk away. And then I completely forget I didn't sign it until someone points it out. \n\nI know it sounds like I'm just being careless, and I'm trying really hard to be better about it. I hope that's not too frustrating to hear? I really want to fix it.\n\nDoctor: When you're working on these tasks, do you have trouble paying attention or concentrating in general, or is it specifically that you lose focus right when you're about to finish something?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess... well, it's not just at the end, if I'm being completely honest. I hope that's okay to admit. I really do try to pay attention the whole time. Like, when I start my shift, I tell myself, \"Okay, you're going to stay completely focused today.\" And I really mean it! \n\nBut... after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking the labels, it just sort of... blurs together? And then I'll suddenly realize I've been staring at the same tray of meat or the same log sheet for I don't know how long, and I'm not even processing what I'm supposed to be doing next. \n\nIt's not that I don't care about the work, I promise. I just... I kind of zone out, I guess? Is that normal? I mean, I know it's probably just me needing to try harder to stay on task, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying focused and finishing tasks, or is this something that's gotten worse recently or started at a particular time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's definitely not a new thing, unfortunately. I really wish I could say it was just the stress of the job, but... I've kind of always been like this. I hope that's not too disappointing to hear? \n\nLike, my mom actually still has some of my old report cards from, gosh, maybe fourth grade? And she was just showing me one recently, and the teacher wrote that I was bright—which was nice of them to say!—but that I needed, um, constant reminders to finish my classwork and actually turn in my homework. \n\nI remember I always understood the lessons, I really did! But I would just... miss big chunks of the instructions unless the teacher was standing right next to me. I guess I've always just needed a little extra help staying on track. Is that... does that help answer your question? I'm sorry if that's not what you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Besides having trouble focusing, do you find that you're often fidgeting, feeling restless, or having a hard time sitting still? Or do people ever tell you that you talk a lot or interrupt them?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm not too fidgety right now? I try really hard to be polite and sit still when I'm supposed to. I don't think I'm restless or anything like that. \n\nAnd I definitely try not to interrupt people! I really value being a good listener. I mean, maybe sometimes I get a little excited and talk a bit fast, but I don't think anyone's ever told me I talk too much or talk over them. I always try to wait my turn. Does that sound okay? I just want to make sure I'm answering right.\n\nDoctor: When you're working or doing other activities, do you ever feel like you need to be moving around, or do you find it hard to stay in one place for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I hope I don't come across that way! I really try my best to just... stay where I'm supposed to be, especially at work. We have our stations, you know? And I know it's important to stay put so everything runs smoothly. \n\nI don't really feel like I *have* to be moving around. I mean, sometimes my feet get a little tired from standing on the mats all day, but I don't get that urge to just get up and pace or anything. I can sit or stand in one place for a long time if that's what I need to do. I really try to be good about that. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making quick decisions or doing things without thinking them through first, or do you usually take time to think before you act?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I'm usually pretty careful? I really try to be, anyway. At work, especially, dealing with the equipment and everything, you have to be so careful, and I always make sure to follow all the safety steps. I wouldn't want to cause an accident or make a mess for anyone else to clean up. \n\nI mean, I guess sometimes if I'm at the store, I might grab a snack I didn't plan on buying, but... I don't really do big things without thinking them through. I try to be really responsible and think about how my actions affect other people. I hope that makes sense? I definitely wouldn't say I'm impulsive or anything bad like that. Does that sound like a normal way to be?\n\nDoctor: When you're not at work, like on your days off or in the evenings, do you find it hard to start and finish tasks around the house or things you want to do for yourself, or is the difficulty mainly happening at work?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I do pretty okay at home? I mean, I really try to keep my place nice and tidy. I wouldn't want anyone to come over and think I'm messy or lazy, so I always make sure to get my chores done on my days off. \n\nSometimes if I'm really tired from the plant, maybe it takes me a little bit of time to get off the couch, but... I don't think it's a big problem or anything. I usually get everything finished because I know I'm supposed to. I really try to be responsible with my own stuff, just like I am at work. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something you really enjoy or find interesting—like a hobby or something you're excited about—do you have the same trouble concentrating and staying focused, or is it mainly when you're doing work tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, I think when it's something I really like doing, I'm... I'm pretty good at it? Like, if I'm reading a book I really love or watching a movie I've been looking forward to, I can usually pay attention just fine. \n\nI really try to focus on the things I enjoy so I don't miss anything good. It's mostly just... well, work can be a little repetitive, you know? With the processing lines and all. So sometimes my mind wanders a bit there, but I really, really try my hardest to stay on task. I wouldn't want you to think I can't focus on *anything*. When it's important to me, or something fun, I do a good job of paying attention, I promise. Is it normal to be able to focus better on fun things? I hope that's a normal way for my brain to work.\n\nDoctor: When you have to do something that's not particularly interesting or fun, but you still need to do it—like paying bills, cleaning, or other chores you don't enjoy—do you have trouble staying focused and finishing those tasks, or do you manage okay with those too?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I think I manage those pretty okay too? I make sure I do them. \n\nI really don't want to be the kind of person who gets behind on things like that. That would be so irresponsible, and I try so hard to be on top of everything. Sometimes, I guess, maybe I'll put on some music or something to make it a little less boring, but I always get it finished. I wouldn't want anyone to think I can't handle my own responsibilities. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Um, yeah... I guess that does happen sometimes. I really, really don't want you to think I'm slacking off, because I promise I start every single shift trying so hard to stay completely locked in. I want to do a perfect job for them. 

But... well, after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking the labels, it just kind of... blurs together? And then I'll suddenly realize I've just been staring at the exact same tray, or my log sheet, and I'm not even taking in what I'm supposed to be doing next. I feel awful when it happens, because I know I should be paying better attention, and I always try to snap right back to it as soon as I notice. 

Does that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding like a bad worker. I really do try my absolute hardest, I promise.

Like, if I'm reading a book I really love or watching a movie I've been looking forward to, I can usually pay attention just fine. \n\nI really try to focus on the things I enjoy so I don't miss anything good. I make sure I do them. \n\nI really don't want to be the kind of person who gets behind on things like that. That would be so irresponsible, and I try so hard to be on top of everything. Sometimes, I guess, maybe I'll put on some music or something to make it a little less boring, but I always get it finished. I wouldn't want anyone to think I can't handle my own responsibilities. I guess that does happen sometimes. I really, really don't want you to think I'm slacking off, because I promise I start every single shift trying so hard to stay completely locked in. I want to do a perfect job for them. \n\nBut... well, after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking the labels, it just kind of... blurs together? I feel awful when it happens, because I know I should be paying better attention, and I always try to snap right back to it as soon as I notice. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding like a bad worker. I just... I kind of zone out, I guess? Is that normal? I mean, I know it's probably just me needing to try harder to stay on task, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying focused and finishing tasks, or is this something that's gotten worse recently or started at a particular time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's definitely not a new thing, unfortunately. I mean, sometimes my feet get a little tired from standing on the mats all day, but I don't get that urge to just get up and pace or anything. I can sit or stand in one place for a long time if that's what I need to do. I really try to be good about that. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making quick decisions or doing things without thinking them through first, or do you usually take time to think before you act?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I'm usually pretty careful? I really try to be, anyway. At work, especially, dealing with the equipment and everything, you have to be so careful, and I always make sure to follow all the safety steps. I wouldn't want to cause an accident or make a mess for anyone else to clean up. \n\nI mean, I guess sometimes if I'm at the store, I might grab a snack I didn't plan on buying, but... I don't really do big things without thinking them through. I try to be really responsible and think about how my actions affect other people. I hope that makes sense? I don't think it's a big problem or anything. I usually get everything finished because I know I'm supposed to. I really try to be responsible with my own stuff, just like I am at work. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I'm pretty good at it? You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, no, not really. I mean, even if I know the answer or I'm excited about something, I still try really hard to keep it in check. I don't want to come across as overbearing or, you know, too loud.

I know there's more to do, but my mind just kind of wanders off to the next thing before I've actually finished the first thing. \n\nLike, I'll be doing the logs, and I know I need to sign the bottom, but then I'll think about how I need to go check the temperature on the cooler, and I just... walk away. And then I completely forget I didn't sign it until someone points it out. \n\nI know it sounds like I'm just being careless, and I'm trying really hard to be better about it. I hope that's not too frustrating to hear? I really want to fix it.\n\nDoctor: When you're working on these tasks, do you have trouble paying attention or concentrating in general, or is it specifically that you lose focus right when you're about to finish something?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess... well, it's not just at the end, if I'm being completely honest. I hope that's okay to admit. I really do try to pay attention the whole time. Like, when I start my shift, I tell myself, \"Okay, you're going to stay completely focused today.\" And I really mean it! \n\nBut... after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking the labels, it just sort of... blurs together? And then I'll suddenly realize I've been staring at the same tray of meat or the same log sheet for I don't know how long, and I'm not even processing what I'm supposed to be doing next. \n\nIt's not that I don't care about the work, I promise. I just... I kind of zone out, I guess? Is that normal? I mean, I know it's probably just me needing to try harder to stay on task, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying focused and finishing tasks, or is this something that's gotten worse recently or started at a particular time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's definitely not a new thing, unfortunately. I really wish I could say it was just the stress of the job, but... I've kind of always been like this. I hope that's not too disappointing to hear? \n\nLike, my mom actually still has some of my old report cards from, gosh, maybe fourth grade? And she was just showing me one recently, and the teacher wrote that I was bright—which was nice of them to say!—but that I needed, um, constant reminders to finish my classwork and actually turn in my homework. \n\nI remember I always understood the lessons, I really did! But I would just... miss big chunks of the instructions unless the teacher was standing right next to me. I guess I've always just needed a little extra help staying on track. Is that... does that help answer your question? I'm sorry if that's not what you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Besides having trouble focusing, do you find that you're often fidgeting, feeling restless, or having a hard time sitting still? Or do people ever tell you that you talk a lot or interrupt them?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm not too fidgety right now? I try really hard to be polite and sit still when I'm supposed to. I don't think I'm restless or anything like that. \n\nAnd I definitely try not to interrupt people! I really value being a good listener. I mean, maybe sometimes I get a little excited and talk a bit fast, but I don't think anyone's ever told me I talk too much or talk over them. I always try to wait my turn. Does that sound okay? And I know it's important to stay put so everything runs smoothly. \n\nI don't really feel like I *have* to be moving around. I mean, sometimes my feet get a little tired from standing on the mats all day, but I don't get that urge to just get up and pace or anything. I can sit or stand in one place for a long time if that's what I need to do. I really try to be good about that. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making quick decisions or doing things without thinking them through first, or do you usually take time to think before you act?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I'm usually pretty careful? I really try to be, anyway. At work, especially, dealing with the equipment and everything, you have to be so careful, and I always make sure to follow all the safety steps. I wouldn't want to cause an accident or make a mess for anyone else to clean up. \n\nI mean, I guess sometimes if I'm at the store, I might grab a snack I didn't plan on buying, but... I don't really do big things without thinking them through. I try to be really responsible and think about how my actions affect other people. I hope that makes sense? Does that sound like a normal way to be?\n\nDoctor: When you're not at work, like on your days off or in the evenings, do you find it hard to start and finish tasks around the house or things you want to do for yourself, or is the difficulty mainly happening at work?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I do pretty okay at home? I'm pretty good at it? Is that the sort of thing I should be here for?\n\nDoctor: When you're doing these tasks at work, do you actually forget that there are steps left to complete, or do you remember there's more to do but just find yourself stopping anyway?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's a little bit of both? Or, well, maybe I just get a bit distracted. I definitely don't mean to just stop, I promise. It's more like... I know there's more to do, but my mind just kind of wanders off to the next thing before I've actually finished the first thing. \n\nLike, I'll be doing the logs, and I know I need to sign the bottom, but then I'll think about how I need to go check the temperature on the cooler, and I just... walk away. And then I completely forget I didn't sign it until someone points it out. \n\nI know it sounds like I'm just being careless, and I'm trying really hard to be better about it. I hope that's not too frustrating to hear? I really want to fix it.\n\nDoctor: When you're working on these tasks, do you have trouble paying attention or concentrating in general, or is it specifically that you lose focus right when you're about to finish something?\n\nPatient: Um, I guess... well, it's not just at the end, if I'm being completely honest. I hope that's okay to admit. I really do try to pay attention the whole time. Like, when I start my shift, I tell myself, \"Okay, you're going to stay completely focused today.\" And I really mean it! \n\nBut... after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking the labels, it just sort of... blurs together? And then I'll suddenly realize I've been staring at the same tray of meat or the same log sheet for I don't know how long, and I'm not even processing what I'm supposed to be doing next. \n\nIt's not that I don't care about the work, I promise. I just... I kind of zone out, I guess? Is that normal? I mean, I know it's probably just me needing to try harder to stay on task, right?\n\nDoctor: Have you always had trouble staying focused and finishing tasks, or is this something that's gotten worse recently or started at a particular time?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's definitely not a new thing, unfortunately. I really wish I could say it was just the stress of the job, but... I've kind of always been like this. I hope that's not too disappointing to hear? \n\nLike, my mom actually still has some of my old report cards from, gosh, maybe fourth grade? And she was just showing me one recently, and the teacher wrote that I was bright—which was nice of them to say!—but that I needed, um, constant reminders to finish my classwork and actually turn in my homework. \n\nI remember I always understood the lessons, I really did! But I would just... miss big chunks of the instructions unless the teacher was standing right next to me. I guess I've always just needed a little extra help staying on track. Is that... does that help answer your question? I'm sorry if that's not what you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: Besides having trouble focusing, do you find that you're often fidgeting, feeling restless, or having a hard time sitting still? Or do people ever tell you that you talk a lot or interrupt them?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, I hope I'm not too fidgety right now? I try really hard to be polite and sit still when I'm supposed to. I don't think I'm restless or anything like that. \n\nAnd I definitely try not to interrupt people! I really value being a good listener. I mean, maybe sometimes I get a little excited and talk a bit fast, but I don't think anyone's ever told me I talk too much or talk over them. I always try to wait my turn. Does that sound okay? I just want to make sure I'm answering right.\n\nDoctor: When you're working or doing other activities, do you ever feel like you need to be moving around, or do you find it hard to stay in one place for long periods of time?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I hope I don't come across that way! I really try my best to just... stay where I'm supposed to be, especially at work. We have our stations, you know? And I know it's important to stay put so everything runs smoothly. \n\nI don't really feel like I *have* to be moving around. I mean, sometimes my feet get a little tired from standing on the mats all day, but I don't get that urge to just get up and pace or anything. I can sit or stand in one place for a long time if that's what I need to do. I really try to be good about that. Is that... is that the kind of thing you were asking about? I hope I'm explaining it right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself making quick decisions or doing things without thinking them through first, or do you usually take time to think before you act?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I'm usually pretty careful? I really try to be, anyway. At work, especially, dealing with the equipment and everything, you have to be so careful, and I always make sure to follow all the safety steps. I wouldn't want to cause an accident or make a mess for anyone else to clean up. \n\nI mean, I guess sometimes if I'm at the store, I might grab a snack I didn't plan on buying, but... I don't really do big things without thinking them through. I try to be really responsible and think about how my actions affect other people. I hope that makes sense? I definitely wouldn't say I'm impulsive or anything bad like that. Does that sound like a normal way to be?\n\nDoctor: When you're not at work, like on your days off or in the evenings, do you find it hard to start and finish tasks around the house or things you want to do for yourself, or is the difficulty mainly happening at work?\n\nPatient: Um, I think I do pretty okay at home? I mean, I really try to keep my place nice and tidy. I wouldn't want anyone to come over and think I'm messy or lazy, so I always make sure to get my chores done on my days off. \n\nSometimes if I'm really tired from the plant, maybe it takes me a little bit of time to get off the couch, but... I don't think it's a big problem or anything. I usually get everything finished because I know I'm supposed to. I really try to be responsible with my own stuff, just like I am at work. Is that... does that answer your question okay? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: When you're doing something you really enjoy or find interesting—like a hobby or something you're excited about—do you have the same trouble concentrating and staying focused, or is it mainly when you're doing work tasks?\n\nPatient: Um, I think when it's something I really like doing, I'm... I'm pretty good at it? Like, if I'm reading a book I really love or watching a movie I've been looking forward to, I can usually pay attention just fine. \n\nI really try to focus on the things I enjoy so I don't miss anything good. It's mostly just... well, work can be a little repetitive, you know? With the processing lines and all. So sometimes my mind wanders a bit there, but I really, really try my hardest to stay on task. I wouldn't want you to think I can't focus on *anything*. When it's important to me, or something fun, I do a good job of paying attention, I promise. Is it normal to be able to focus better on fun things? I hope that's a normal way for my brain to work.\n\nDoctor: When you have to do something that's not particularly interesting or fun, but you still need to do it—like paying bills, cleaning, or other chores you don't enjoy—do you have trouble staying focused and finishing those tasks, or do you manage okay with those too?\n\nPatient: Um, well, I think I manage those pretty okay too? I mean, nobody really *likes* paying bills or doing chores, right? But I know they have to get done, so I just... I make sure I do them. \n\nI really don't want to be the kind of person who gets behind on things like that. That would be so irresponsible, and I try so hard to be on top of everything. Sometimes, I guess, maybe I'll put on some music or something to make it a little less boring, but I always get it finished. I wouldn't want anyone to think I can't handle my own responsibilities. I do a good job of taking care of my stuff, I really do. Does that sound like I'm doing it right? I hope I'm explaining things the way you need me to.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you can focus well on things you enjoy and that you manage to finish chores and bills. But at work, even though you know it's important for your job, you still have trouble staying focused on the processing tasks. When something is important but also boring or repetitive—like the work tasks—do you find your mind wandering even though you really want to focus and you know you should?\n\nPatient: Um, yeah... I guess that does happen sometimes. I really, really don't want you to think I'm slacking off, because I promise I start every single shift trying so hard to stay completely locked in. I want to do a perfect job for them. \n\nBut... well, after a while, all the cutting and weighing and checking the labels, it just kind of... blurs together? And then I'll suddenly realize I've just been staring at the exact same tray, or my log sheet, and I'm not even taking in what I'm supposed to be doing next. I feel awful when it happens, because I know I should be paying better attention, and I always try to snap right back to it as soon as I notice. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm not sounding like a bad worker. I really do try my absolute hardest, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like you have excess energy or feel restless inside, even if you're sitting still on the outside? Like, does your mind feel like it's racing or jumping around a lot, even when your body is calm?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think so? I mean, I don't feel like I have all this extra energy or anything like that. I'm actually pretty calm most of the time. \n\nI wouldn't want you to think I'm hyper or bouncing off the walls or anything. I can sit still just fine, I promise. I don't fidget or disrupt things. And my mind... well, I guess it wanders a little bit, like I said about work, but it doesn't feel like it's racing or out of control. It's just normal thinking, I think? \n\nI really try to keep myself collected and professional. Is that the right answer? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. I don't feel restless like that at all.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you try not to interrupt people and that you wait your turn. But I'm curious—when you're in conversations or meetings, do you ever find it hard to wait for your turn to speak, or do words just kind of come out before you've thought about what you're going to say?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I do that. I really try to be polite and listen to people when they're talking. \n\nI know it's really rude to just blurt things out or talk over someone, so I always make sure to wait until they're completely finished before I say anything. I wouldn't want anyone to think I was being disrespectful or not caring about what they had to say. Even if I have a thought, I just keep it in until it's the right time. I try really hard to be a good listener. \n\nI hope that's okay? I just always try to be mindful of other people in conversations.\n\nDoctor: You mentioned you try to wait your turn in conversations. But I'm wondering—in situations where you're excited or know the answer to something, do you ever find it hard to hold back and end up talking a lot or saying things quickly, even if you didn't plan to?\n\nPatient: Um, no, not really. I mean, even if I know the answer or I'm excited about something, I still try really hard to keep it in check. I don't want to come across as overbearing or, you know, too loud. \n\nI'm pretty good at just staying quiet and letting other people have their moment. I'd hate to be the person who just takes over a conversation or talks too fast for people to follow. I always try to be considerate of the room. I don't talk too much, I promise. I try to be very thoughtful about what I say. \n\nIs that what you mean? I just want to make sure I'm answering this right. I don't think I do that at all.\n\nDoctor: When you're sitting at work or at home trying to focus on something, do you ever feel like there's a lot of activity or movement happening inside your body or mind—like your thoughts are moving really fast or your body wants to move around—even if you're not actually moving around?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I really don't think so. I mean, when I'm at work—I work at a meat processing plant, so I have to be really focused and careful with what I'm doing—I don't feel like my body is, you know, wanting to move around or anything. I just stand at my station and do my job. \n\nAnd my mind, well... I guess I mentioned before that it wanders sometimes, but it doesn't feel like it's moving super fast or anything crazy like that. It's just normal thoughts, I think. I really try to stay focused on the task in front of me so I don't make any mistakes. I definitely don't feel like there's a lot of activity going on inside that shouldn't be there. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm trying to be as clear as possible. I just don't want you to think I'm, like, out of control or anything. I'm actually very steady. Is that a good answer?\n\nDoctor: When you're at work standing at your station for long periods, or even when you're sitting down, do you ever feel like you just need to move around—like your legs feel restless or twitchy, or you get an urge to get up and walk around—even if you manage to stay in place?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I don't think I get like that. I mean, standing in one spot for my whole shift is just part of the job, so I'm really used to it. I definitely don't get twitchy legs or feel like I have to walk away from my station. \n\nI know that would look really bad, like I wasn't dedicated to my work or like I couldn't handle it, so I just make sure to stay right where I'm supposed to be. I really don't feel restless at all. I just do my best to stay still and do a good job. I'd never want to cause a problem on the floor by wandering off. \n\nIs that... is that what most people do? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of answers. I hope it's okay that I don't have that problem.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."
